# Skills · C · part 37

873 entries, clip-cutter to closure-report. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **clip-cutter**: Cuts, reorders, and joins attached video into a finished MP4 from a content-reverse-engineer EDL (source in/out segments + overlays). Accepts one video or several smaller parts that make up one recording, stitches them into one source timeline, tightens cut points against the audio, verifies runtime, and returns the file plus a corrected overlay map. Triggers on: a video (or video parts) attached with a clip package or EDL, "cut this", "trim the video", "build the cut", "render clip N", "make the MP4", "stitch these parts", "burn in the overlays". ([Adaakal/lifestyleos-marketplace](https://github.com/Adaakal/lifestyleos-marketplace/tree/HEAD/content-repurposer/skills/clip-cutter))
- **clip-decomposition**: Plan executable clip boundaries while preserving canonical coverage and action. Use for Generation Clip Plan authoring and review; no media generation. ([zuimengyz/drama-plugin](https://github.com/zuimengyz/drama-plugin/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/clip-decomposition) · ★3)
- **clip-dissect**: Use this skill when you need to automatically describe or interpret the functionality of individual neurons in deep neural networks (DNNs) using CLIP-based semantic analysis, perform mechanistic interpretability research on vision models, dissect convolutional or transformer-based image classifiers, identify what visual concepts activate specific neurons, or compare neuron descriptions across different probing datasets and concept sets. - **closing-work**: The /flow engine's DONE stage — report completion on a work item, move it to Done with the agent/completed label, create any follow-up work, run a project pulse check for the next loop action, and clean up the worktree. Use when running /flow:done or advancing an approved work item into the DONE stage. ([dork-labs/marketplace](https://github.com/dork-labs/marketplace/tree/HEAD/plugins/flow/skills/closing-work))
- **closure**: Apply the Gestalt principle of Closure — the perceptual tendency to see incomplete shapes as whole. Use when designing icons (a few strokes can imply a closed shape), illustrations (negative space defines forms), logos (the IBM stripes, the WWF panda, the FedEx arrow), card layouts (cards don't need full borders to feel like containers), or any visual that benefits from suggested rather than fully drawn shapes. Closure lets you communicate more with less; the user's perception completes the form. ([Deibler/universal-design-principles](https://github.com/Deibler/universal-design-principles/tree/HEAD/plugins/perception-and-hierarchy-principles/skills/closure) · ★8)
- **closure**: Use when reasoning about closure — Use to decide a candidate against an open Millennium Problem. A conjecture has two exits — a proof, which is not a computation, and a refutation, which for several of these IS one. Implements the deciders: zeta evaluates ζ by Borwein's algorithm (verified against ζ(2)=π²/6, ζ(4)=π⁴/90 and the first six known zeros) so refutesRiemann rules on a candidate off-line zero; satisfies checks a SAT certificate exactly so refutesSolver catches a bluffing, wrong-certificate or wrong-verdict solver; refutesBSD compares the two ranks. Three of the seven have no finite c… ([erpax/erpax](https://github.com/erpax/erpax/tree/HEAD/src/millennium/closure) · ★2)
- **closure**: Use when an external dependency must fall back to erpax ITSELF — every external role (payment-provider, signing, notification, search, federation) has a registered internal provider that completes the operation when the external call fails (Conservation Law 53, withInternalFallback). The dependency graph terminates at erpax; everything falls back to the self. ([erpax/erpax](https://github.com/erpax/erpax/tree/HEAD/src/self/closure) · ★2)

- **closure**: El cerebro completa formas incompletas. Use cuando diseñe iconos minimalistas, logos, o interfaces que sugieren más de lo que muestran explícitamente. - **closure**: Use when a work session is wrapping up or about to end — the user signals closing, e.g. 「本 session 還有未盡事宜嗎」「這個 session 還有未盡事宜嗎」「還有未盡事宜嗎」「還有未完成的事嗎」「還有未完成的事項嗎」「還有沒完成的事嗎」「還有什麼沒做/沒處理的嗎」「收尾」「未盡事宜」「結案」「收工」, or (en) "anything left?", "are we done?", "what's left?", "wrap up", "loose ends" — or before you end a long or multi-thread session. Covers session loose ends, outstanding/unresolved items, undocumented decisions, work left unhanded-off. ([howar31/closure](https://github.com/howar31/closure/tree/HEAD/skills/closure))
- **Closure Scope Retention Guard**: Detects long-lived objects (caches, listeners, singletons) that capture an entire enclosing scope via closures/environment capture, keeping large arrays or buffers alive, and proposes copying only the needed fields. - **closure-check**: Gate whether a working set of atoms/clusters is closed under dependency edges (OPEN lists crossings). Use after organize-work when the border must be a real min-cut. Not organize-work. Not architecture-design. ([kyzobuild/kyzo](https://github.com/kyzobuild/kyzo/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/closure-check))
- **closure-completion-cost**: Recognize when closure is the wrong choice — when the perceptual completion work it asks of the user is too costly, ambiguous, or risky. Use when designing for accessibility audiences, novice users, high-stakes interfaces (medical, financial, safety), small rendering sizes that strain perception, or any context where the user shouldn't have to do completion work to understand the form. The skill is judging when explicit forms serve the user better than suggested ones, even at the cost of visual elegance. ([Deibler/universal-design-principles](https://github.com/Deibler/universal-design-principles/tree/HEAD/plugins/perception-and-hierarchy-principles/skills/closure-completion-cost) · ★8)
- **closure-first-overnight-batch**: Run a high-leverage overnight batch by clearing stale-open approved issues first, converting shared blockers into tracked issues, and reserving only one lane for true implementation. ([vamseeachanta/workspace-hub](https://github.com/vamseeachanta/workspace-hub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/workspace-hub-learned/closure-first-overnight-batch) · ★17 · 2 copies)
- **closure-implied-shapes**: Design forms that exploit closure — suggesting shapes through partial outlines, negative space, or strategic gaps so the user's perception completes them. Use when designing icons that need to read at small sizes, logos that need memorability, illustrations with restraint, card layouts with implied containment, or charts with implied structure. The art is suggesting just enough that the user perceives the whole, without leaving so little that they perceive nothing. ([Deibler/universal-design-principles](https://github.com/Deibler/universal-design-principles/tree/HEAD/plugins/perception-and-hierarchy-principles/skills/closure-implied-shapes) · ★8)

Owns the closure→build transition. ([MrBinnacle/skills](https://github.com/MrBinnacle/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/closure-mode))

Invoke when the project has reached final deliverable acceptance and requires formal closure documentation.
